# Appendix A — Verified API & live-frame catalog

_Bind only to these; everything else is a backend gap._

## VERIFIED REST ENDPOINTS (bind only to these)
GET (WebSocket) /api/live — WebSocket upgrade for live frames (stream appends, turn phases/tokens, dream/maintenance ticks, reset).
GET /api/state — Runtime snapshot: active session, audiences, counts, current mood, version.
GET /api/sessions — List all sessions.
GET /api/entities/creator — Get the current creator entity (or null).
POST /api/entities/creator — Resolve/create an entity by name and mark it as creator.
POST /api/entities/:id/borg-role — Set or clear an entity's borg role (creator | null).
POST /api/entities — Create/resolve a generic entity by name and optional kind.
POST /api/sessions/operator — Ensure (create if missing) the demo operator session bound to the creator.
POST /api/sessions/:id/participation — Set a session's participation policy (active/paused/observing/muted).
GET /api/stream — Read the append-only session stream with filtering.
GET /api/turns — Bounded recent turn history (anchor-based reverse scan).
GET /api/turns/:id/ledger — Evidence ledger for a given turn id.
GET /api/memory/bands — Overview of the eight memory bands.
GET /api/semantic/graph — Semantic graph snapshot (top nodes by edge degree + their edges).
GET /api/semantic/nodes/:id — Get a single semantic node.
GET /api/semantic/edges/:id — Get a single semantic edge.
GET /api/memory/bands/:id — Detail/browse/search for one memory band.
GET /api/commitments — List commitments with audience/state/enforcement filters.
GET /api/creator-directives — List creator directives by status.
POST /api/creator-directives/:id/revoke — Revoke a creator directive.
POST /api/creator-directives/:id/supersede — Supersede a creator directive with a replacement.
GET /api/reviews — List review-queue rows (optionally open-only, optionally by kind).
PATCH /api/reviews/:id — Resolve a generic review item (accept/reject/keep/etc per REVIEW_RESOLUTIONS).
POST /api/reviews/:id/creator-directive-reconciliation — Resolve a creator-directive-reconciliation review (supersede-to-survivor or keep).
POST /api/commitments — Create an operator commitment (advisory enforcement).
POST /api/commitments/:id/revoke — Revoke a commitment.
GET /api/shared-state — Shared-state entries for an audience (default 'self').
GET /api/identity — Self/identity snapshot (values, goals, traits, open questions, growth, autobiography).
GET /api/dream/audit — List recent maintenance/dream audit rows.
POST /api/dream/audit/:id/revert — Revert an applied maintenance/dream audit row.
GET /api/dream/state — Dream/offline maintenance state (process health, intervals, pending work).
GET /api/correction/reviews — List open correction-kind review rows.
GET /api/correction/:id/why — Explain provenance/evidence for an object id.
POST /api/correction/:id/forget — Forget/archive/revoke a memory object by id.
POST /api/correction/:id/correct — Queue a manual correction (JSON patch) on an object id.
POST /api/correction/semantic-edges/:id/invalidate — Invalidate a semantic edge.
PATCH /api/correction/reviews/:id — Accept or reject a correction review item.
POST /api/dream/plan — Build + preview a maintenance plan (no mutation).
POST /api/dream/apply — Apply a maintenance plan (by plan_id or fresh).
POST /api/identity/values — Add an identity value.
POST /api/identity/goals — Add an identity goal.
PATCH /api/identity/goals/:id — Update a goal (complete | block | progress).
POST /api/identity/growth-markers — Add a growth marker (with stream evidence event).
PATCH /api/identity/open-questions/:id — Resolve | abandon | bump an open question.
PATCH /api/dream/review/:id — Dismiss a belief-revision (dream) review row.
GET /api/attachments — Batch attachment status lookup (no bytes).
GET /api/attachments/:id — Attachment metadata + perception + status.
GET /api/attachments/:id/bytes — Raw attachment bytes (audience-scoped).
POST /api/turn — Submit a user turn (text + optional image attachments) and enqueue for processing.
GET /api/prompts — List prompt blocks (default/composed/overridden).
GET /api/prompts/assembled — Preview the fully assembled framing prompt.
PUT /api/prompts/:key — Set/override a prompt block by key.
DELETE /api/prompts/:key — Clear a prompt override (reset to default).
POST /api/admin/reset — Wipe + reopen the demo data dir (destructive).

## VERIFIED LIVE/WS FRAMES (bind only to these)
stream:append (when: Fires whenever the engine appends one or more StreamEntry rows to the conversation stream (user_msg, agent_msg, agent_suppressed, agent_observed, thought, tool_call, tool_result, perception, internal_event, dream_report, user_image_attachment). Built in LiveBroadcaster.streamAppend, called from createLiveBridge.onStreamAppend, which the Borg engine drives via the onStreamAppend hook (wired in reset.ts/index.ts).) [type="stream:append", ts:number, session_id?:SessionId (omitted if entries[0] has none; taken from entries[0].session_id), entries:StreamEntry[] -- each: id, timestamp, entry_index?, kind (StreamEntryKind enum: user_msg|user_image_attachment|agent_msg|agent_suppressed|agent_observed|thought|tool_call|tool_result|perception|internal_event|dream_report), content(unknown), turn_id?, turn_status?(active|aborted), token_estimate?, tool_calls?[], audience?, sender_entity_id(nullable), reply_target_entity_id(nullable), source_message_key?, response_to?, persistence_class?(assistant_self_report), receipt_pending?, session_id, compressed]
turn:phase:started (when: WsBridgeTracer maps core event turn_phase.started. Fires at the START of each cognitive turn phase via traceTurnPhase() in phase-trace.ts. Also records currentPhaseByTurnId so later detail events without an explicit phase can be attributed. Session-scoped (delivered to subscribers of data.session_id).) [type="turn:phase:started", ts:number, event="turn_phase.started", data: { turnId, turn_id, session_id?, phase(TurnPhaseName), ts, sub? } (session_id read from data.session_id for routing)]
turn:phase:completed (when: WsBridgeTracer maps core event turn_phase.completed. Fires when a phase finishes successfully in traceTurnPhase(). Carries duration_ms.) [type="turn:phase:completed", ts:number, event="turn_phase.completed", data: { turnId, turn_id, session_id?, phase, ts, duration_ms, sub?(completedSub result) }]
turn:phase:failed (when: WsBridgeTracer maps core event turn_phase.failed. Fires when a phase throws in traceTurnPhase() (error is re-thrown). sub carries 'ErrName: message'.) [type="turn:phase:failed", ts:number, event="turn_phase.failed", data: { turnId, turn_id, session_id?, phase, ts, duration_ms, sub(error string) }]
turn:token (when: WsBridgeTracer maps core event turn.token. Streaming generation: fires per token/chunk during deliberation and final generation via emitTurnTokenTrace(). Only emitted when chunk_text is non-empty. phase is 'delib' (S2 planner / deliberator streaming) or 'final' (finalizer / final generation).) [type="turn:token", ts:number, turn_id:string, session_id?:string, phase:string (TurnTokenTracePhase: delib|final), chunk_text:string, sequence:number]
turn:token:flush (when: WsBridgeTracer maps core event turn.token.flush. Fires once at end of a streamed segment via emitTurnTokenFlushTrace(), delivering the full accumulated text for that phase (lets the UI replace incremental tokens with canonical text).) [type="turn:token:flush", ts:number, turn_id:string, session_id?:string, phase:string (delib|final), full_text:string]
turn:terminal (when: WsBridgeTracer maps core event turn.terminal. Fires once when a turn reaches a terminal state, emitted by turn-orchestrator.ts. Also clears currentPhaseByTurnId for that turn. outcome (TurnTerminalOutcome) lives inside data.) [type="turn:terminal", ts:number, event="turn.terminal", data: { turnId, turn_id, session_id, outcome (reflected|suppressed_closure|suppressed_generation_gate|suppressed_action|aborted|error), ts, duration_ms }]
evidence_ledger:built (when: WsBridgeTracer maps core event evidence_ledger.built (only when turnId present). Fires when the per-turn evidence ledger is assembled. SIDE EFFECT: caches ledger in ledgerCache keyed by turn_id (served by the ledger HTTP endpoint; cleared on reset).) [type="evidence_ledger:built", ts:number, turn_id:string, session_id?:string, ledger: data.ledger (full evidence-ledger object, or null)]
turn:delib_path (when: WsBridgeTracer maps core event deliberation.path.completed (only when turnId present AND data.path is exactly 'system_1' or 'system_2'). Fires when the deliberator settles on a System-1 (fast) vs System-2 (deliberate) path.) [type="turn:delib_path", ts:number, turn_id:string, session_id?:string, path: "system_1"|"system_2"]
turn:final_attempt (when: WsBridgeTracer maps core event commitment_guard.regeneration_requested (only when turnId present). Each occurrence is a finalizer RE-attempt after a commitment-guard regeneration request; the first attempt is implicit (no event). Always emits attempt:2 (a fixed increment hint for the UI, not a real attempt counter).) [type="turn:final_attempt", ts:number, turn_id:string, session_id?:string, attempt: 2 (hardcoded)]
dream:process:started (when: WsBridgeTracer maps core event offline_process.started (only when data.process_name is a string). Fires when an offline maintenance/dream process begins (consolidator, curator, reflector, semantic-extractor, ruminator, review-resolver, etc.). NOTE: this frame carries NO session_id, so it is routed via frameSessionId -> undefined and delivered to GLOBAL subscribers only.) [type="dream:process:started", ts:number, process:string (OfflineProcessName, from data.process_name), run_id:string|null (from data.turnId), phase: data.phase coerced to 'plan'|'apply' (defaults 'apply')]
dream:process:completed (when: WsBridgeTracer maps core event offline_process.completed (only when data.process_name is a string). Fires when an offline/dream process finishes. Also GLOBAL-only delivery (no session_id).) [type="dream:process:completed", ts:number, process:string (OfflineProcessName), run_id:string|null (data.turnId), phase:'plan'|'apply' (default 'apply'), duration_ms?:number, errors:number (default 0), candidates_accepted:number (default 0)]
turn:phase:detail (when: WsBridgeTracer CATCH-ALL: any tracer event with a turnId that is not one of the explicitly-handled events above falls through to this frame. This is how the long TurnTraceEventName taxonomy (perception.*, retrieval.*, shared_state.*, semantic_revision.*, deliberation.*, llm_call.*, tool_call.*, extraction.*, action_*, frame_anomaly.*, closure_loop.*, open_question_resolution.*, reflector.*, commitment_guard.* (non-regeneration), commitment_check.completed, closure_response_guard.completed, internal_identifier_guard.completed, citation.*, attachment.*, etc.) reaches the UI. phase is data.phase or the last-known phase for the turn (currentPhaseByTurnId). The verbose data is reduced to a one-line 'summary' (key=value pairs, skipping turnId/turn_id/session_id/ts/phase/duration_ms; strings truncated at 60 chars, summary capped at 200).) [type="turn:phase:detail", ts:number, turn_id:string, session_id?:string, phase?:string (explicit or remembered, omitted if unknown), event:TurnTraceEventName (the raw fall-through event name), summary:string (compact human-readable digest, '(no detail)' if empty)]
maintenance:tick (when: NOT from the tracer -- broadcast directly. broadcastMaintenanceTick() in app.ts builds and emits it; wired via wireMaintenanceSchedulerLiveObserver -> scheduler.setObserver.onTick (cadence-driven light/heavy) and on error/manual ('manual' cadence, status 'error'). BROADCAST GLOBALLY to all clients (deliverToAll=true in broadcaster.broadcast; bypasses session routing/buffering).) [type="maintenance:tick", ts:number, cadence: MaintenanceCadence | "manual", status: MaintenanceTickResult['status'] | "error", processes: OfflineProcessName[] (from result.results map), changed:boolean (changes>0), changes:number, errors:number, pending_extraction_episodes?:number, run_id?:string|null (present iff result!=null), duration_ms?:number (rounded), reason?:string]
borg:reset (when: NOT from the tracer -- broadcast directly at the END of the reset flow in createResetBorgController (reset.ts), after the data dir is wiped and a fresh Borg is reopened. Side effects in that flow: clearAllSessionBuffers() at start, ledgerCache.clear() and resetTraceState() just before this frame. BROADCAST GLOBALLY (deliverToAll=true; bypasses session routing). Signals clients to clear all turn/session state and re-fetch.) [type="borg:reset", ts:number]

## Endpoints that exist but were absent from the original proposal

- POST /api/entities -- create/resolve a generic entity (name + optional kind). The proposal lists /api/entities only as a GET-style resource under Core runtime/Governance and never mentions creating an entity via POST.
- POST /api/sessions/operator -- ensure/create the operator session. The proposal mentions an 'open operator session' control in Mission Control UX but never lists this endpoint in section 2 or any 'Primary backend inputs' block.
- POST /api/identity/values -- add an identity value. The proposal's Identity Studio lists /api/identity/values as an input and describes an 'add value' action, but only ever as a generic resource; the create endpoint (POST) is not enumerated and there is no GET /api/identity/values endpoint (values come bundled in GET /api/identity).
- POST /api/identity/goals -- add an identity goal. Same as values: proposal references /api/identity/goals and an 'add' action but does not enumerate the POST create endpoint; there is no standalone GET /api/identity/goals (goals come from GET /api/identity).
- POST /api/identity/growth-markers -- add a growth marker. Proposal lists /api/identity/growth-markers as an input but does not note it is POST-only-create (no GET); growth markers are read via GET /api/identity.
- WebSocket subscription protocol on /api/live -- the proposal treats /api/live purely as a one-way live-frame feed. The handler also accepts inbound client SUBSCRIPTION messages (broadcaster.handleSubscriptionMessage), which the proposal never mentions.
- CLARIFICATION on commitment reconciliation: the proposal lists `/api/reviews?kind=commitment_reconciliation` and designs a 'Commitment reconciliation' repair flow with survivor/revoke/keep options. The listing filter works (it is a valid review kind), but NO dedicated resolution endpoint exists -- unlike creator directives, there is no POST /api/reviews/:id/commitment-reconciliation. Commitment-reconciliation rows would have to be resolved through the generic PATCH /api/reviews/:id. This is the closest thing in the proposal to a route that does not exist as described.
- CLARIFICATION on /api/dream/review/:id: the proposal's Dream Ops and Review & Repair sections imply general review actions, but this PATCH endpoint ONLY accepts action:'dismiss' (belief-revision rows). The proposal's broad 'Generic actions: keep both / supersede / invalidate / dismiss / accept / reject / keep / weaken / archive node' list is not all wired to a single endpoint -- they are split across PATCH /api/reviews/:id, PATCH /api/correction/reviews/:id, POST .../creator-directive-reconciliation, POST .../semantic-edges/:id/invalidate, and this dismiss-only dream route.
- NO endpoints the proposal lists are outright fabricated/non-existent. Every concrete path string in response.md sections 2 and the per-screen inputs maps to a real route (allowing for the commitment-reconciliation caveat above). The proposal's gaps are omissions of real routes, not invented ones.

# Appendix B — Adversarial feasibility & cardinal-rule review

- **Global Shell — routing, left rail, top context strip, global session + live cache** — severity: major; missing pivots: Inspector pivot for SESSION rows: the spec's InspectorRef union lists 'session' as a kind and says the fleet is a valid pivot source, but the fleet's row click is bound to setSessionId (navigation) and the kebab only lists policy/copy/open-in-stream -- there is no described path to openInspector({kind:'session', id}). If sessions are inspectable objects, the fleet needs an explicit pivot affordance (e.g. kebab 'inspect session'), otherwise 'session' is a dead Inspector kind from this host., Inspector pivot for CREATOR/ENTITY is described (creator pill -> openInspector entity) but the 'mark creator' form result and the audience pill have no pivot. The audience pill names an audience that maps to an entity (audience_entity_id is on SessionRecord and is already prop-drilled to Cognition); pivoting the audience pill to openInspector({kind:'entity', id: activeSession.audience_entity_id}) is an obvious omitted seam., No pivot out of the RuntimeStrip telemetry segments. The strip shows dream/maintenance/review/questions/commit counts; clicking the review/commit/questions segments to setView (review/governance/identity) -- the natural mirror of the rail-badge->setView behavior the spec already defines -- is not specified, leaving the bottom strip non-interactive while the top strip and rail badges are pivot sources.
  - Verified against the live tree (demo/web/src and demo/server/src/live.ts), not just the catalog.

FABRICATION CHECK: clean. All 11 apiBindings appear verbatim in the verified REST catalog (GET /api/state, /api/sessions, /api/entities/creator, /api/dream/state, /api/prompts, /api/commitments, /api/reviews; POST /api/entities/creator, /api/sessions/operator, /api/sessions/:id/participation, /api/admin/reset). All 5 liveFrameBindings (stream:append, maintenance:tick, dream:process:started, dream:process:completed, borg:reset) are real frame types defined in demo/web/src/api/types.ts (lines 1125-1216) and match the catalog. Every structural field the design keys on is real: StateSnapshot.counts.{turns,commitments,open_qs,open_reviews,dream_audit_rows} (types.ts:1001-1006), current_mood/version/audiences/active_session, SessionRecord.{participation_policy,audience_role,last_turn_id,status,audience_label,conversation_kind,source_type} (types.ts:1037-1051), CommitmentItem.enforcement_class==='critical'/state==='active' (CommitmentEnforcement='critical'|'advisory', CommitmentState='active'|'revoked'|'expired'), getReviews({openOnly,kind}) with ReviewKind including creator_directive_reconciliation/commitment_reconciliation (types.ts:681-682), DreamProcessSummary.last_status='ok'|'error'|null (types.ts:774), PromptBlockView.overridden (types.ts:1249). The reused-pattern claims also check out: useSession's replaceState/popstate/default-deletes-param idiom (use-session.ts), Rail RouteId/RAIL_ITEMS/aria-label contract (Rail.tsx), Topbar wsLabel/wsToneClass/.topbar-pill vocab, StatusBar moodLabel/countValue/maintenanceTickLabel/Tone, SessionsSidebar relativeTime/shortId/previewLine/sourceLabel/.session-row.active/.dot.alive, ParticipationPolicyControl with a 'locked' prop (Cognition/index.tsx:152-207), setSessionPolicy client method, and useLiveEvents as a Set<handler> fan-out so a second shell subscriber needs no extra socket (use-live-events.ts:39,120). Two minor copy nits, non-blocking: (1) ResetButton lives in .topbar-end today, not .topbar-pills; (2) today's breadcrumb renders session_id directly, the spec upgrades it to a session label -- fine, just not 'verbatim'.

CARDINAL-RULE CHECK: clean. (a) No recall/memory gating by audience/session: the shell explicitly passes session/audience/role to screens as PROPS for disclosure-labeling and never as a memory-view filter; the strip pills (audience/role/policy/creator/mood) are stated as label-only with 'must not let role gate anything'. The SessionFleet status/role filters are correctly scoped to the navigation roster only ('do NOT touch what the entity recalls and do not filter any memory screen') -- this is UI list filtering of a session picker, the allowed kind, not a cognition gate. (b) No output policing: the shell never inspects/rewrites/suppresses model output; ResetButton and the policy write are host-boundary action controls, not output filters. (c) No disclosure-as-recall-filter: provenance/labels are render-time pills and ranking annotations only. Participation-policy writes are correctly framed as action-policy, not recall gates, and mirror the existing endpoint/lock rule. All five badge derivations key on enum structural fields (state, enforcement_class, last_status, overridden, status) with zero content/word matching -- language-agnostic, consistent with the multilingual constraint. The mood glyph is derived from numeric valence/arousal sign+magnitude, not from any word. Opus 5.0 verdict (in-scope: pure operator-UI presentation/navigation + shared live-cache plumbing) is correct.

SEVERITY = major solely because of the undeclared transport gap: the per-session-recency live pulse (a stated P0 'substrate' feature -- 'each fleet row's recency pulse without a per-session socket') is built on a false assumption about WS routing and will silently work for only the active session under current code, with no backend-gap acknowledgment. Everything else is clean: no fabricated endpoints, no cardinal-rule violation. If the per-row pulse is descoped to active-session-only (or the client adds subscribe_global / the fleet derives recency from last_activity_at refetch deltas), the residual severity drops to minor (the three omitted Inspector pivots)."
- **Universal Inspector drawer — the core cross-linking primitive** — severity: minor; missing pivots: Dream/offline maintenance state pivot: catalog exposes GET /api/dream/state (getDreamState) but the Inspector binds only GET /api/dream/audit. A dream-audit-row Inspector should be able to pivot into / annotate with the run's process-health/interval/pending-work state from getDreamState. Minor omission — adjacent and verified-available., Attachment -> owning stream-entry / turn pivot: the attachment object resolves via getAttachmentMetadata but the Relationships tab lists no pivot back to the stream entry (user_image_attachment) or turn that carried it, even though that linkage is the obvious 'show related stream entries' direction for an attachment., Entity -> authored stream entries / commitments / directives pivot: the 'entity' row type resolves via getCreatorEntity / row sender_entity_id but the spec defines no outbound pivots from an entity (e.g. to commitments scoped to that audience via getCommitments({audience}) as a DISCLOSURE/browse filter, or to directives). Entity is left as a near-dead-end node., prompt block -> assembled-prompt pivot is implied (getAssembledPrompt is bound) but not surfaced as an explicit Relationships/Timeline pivot showing which assembled framing a given block contributes to.
  - FABRICATION: Clean. All 41 apiBindings and all 11 liveFrameBindings appear verbatim (method+path / frame type) in the verified catalog — diffed programmatically, zero fabrications. Every client fn named in the resolver table (getSharedState, getMemoryBand, getSemanticNode/Edge, getWhy, postCorrection*, patchGoal, patchOpenQuestion, revertDreamAudit, putPrompt/deletePrompt, setSessionPolicy, getAttachmentMetadata, attachmentBytesUrl, etc.) exists in demo/web/src/api/client.ts with the cited signatures. The genuinely-new endpoints are correctly quarantined in requiresBackendGap, not smuggled into bindings.

CARDINAL RULES: Clean — no violations. (a) No recall gating: the Inspector is explicitly framed as an OPERATOR-CONSOLE read surface that "surfaces what the entity KNOWS with disclosure/provenance/taint/audience rendered as labels, never as recall filters"; Evidence tab states "nothing is filtered by audience"; Disclosure state says "every recalled row renders its DisclosureLabels; nothing is hidden by current audience" and combined-label unknown is "shown as a neutral chip, never demoted to public" — matches the CARDINAL MEMORY RULE fail-closed semantics. The audience params that exist (getSharedState(audience), optional getCommitments({audience})) are operator-facing DISCLOSURE/browse reads of shared-state-per-audience and the action-auth commitment filter — not the entity's cognition recall path — so they are labels/browse-scopes, not cognition gates. (b) No output policing: Actions tab exposes only the sanctioned correction/review/governance mutations (correct/forget/invalidate/revoke/supersede/resolve) that queue reviews or mutate governance state — it never rewrites or suppresses the entity's user-facing output. (c) DisclosureLabels is a pure render-time Tag row. The Opus 5.0 verdict (in-scope, presentation/cross-linking/labeling) is accurate.

INACCURATE REUSE CLAIMS (the basis for minor severity — implementation honesty, not API fabrication):
1. SemanticEdgeDetail.tsx does NOT exist. Spec says 'SemanticNodeDetail.tsx and SemanticEdgeDetail.tsx ... are already clean standalone detail cards' dropped into the Summary tab. Only SemanticNodeDetail.tsx exists (handles SemanticMemoryNode only). Semantic edges are currently rendered INLINE inside demo/web/src/screens/Memory/index.tsx (lines ~168-178), not as an extractable component. So the edge Summary tab requires NEW component work, not 'reuse verbatim'.
2. OpenQuestionEventsSection is a NON-exported local function inside demo/web/src/screens/Identity/index.tsx (line 256), not a reusable export. The Timeline tab 'reuses the pattern' — fine as a pattern, but it must be extracted/lifted, not imported as-is. Spec lists it under 'Reused, not rewritten' which overstates availability.
3. WhyDrawer's JsonValueView is also a non-exported local fn (WhyDrawer.tsx line 21). The spec's word 'lifted' is honest (implies extraction), but combined with #1/#2 there are at least three 'reuse' items that actually require extraction-to-shared-module work the migration note under-scopes.

VERIFIED-CORRECT claims worth noting as load-bearing: correctionActionKind in Memory/index.tsx uses exactly the ep_/semn_/seme_ prefixes the spec generalizes into sniffType; RouteId in Rail.tsx contains all sourceRoute targets the spec names (cognition/stream/memory/identity/commit/directives/review/dream/prompts); LedgerView({turnId,cachedLedger,active,audience}) does graceful 404 ('ledger not retained pre-restart') exactly as the spec relies on for the Evidence tab + the persisted-trace gap; shared-state IS surfaced under the Directives screen (no separate Shared route), so shared-state entry -> directives sourceRoute is correct; use-turn-stream exposes flowSnapshotByTurn/ledgerByTurn/eventTail as claimed; live-context exports useLiveEventsContext/LiveEventsProvider. The empty Graph/ and Shared/ screen dirs are unused stubs and the spec correctly does not depend on them.

Relevant files: demo/web/src/api/client.ts; demo/web/src/screens/Memory/index.tsx (correctionActionKind, inline edge render); demo/web/src/screens/Identity/index.tsx (OpenQuestionEventsSection); demo/web/src/screens/Cognition/LedgerView.tsx; demo/web/src/components/WhyDrawer.tsx; demo/web/src/components/SemanticNodeDetail.tsx; demo/web/src/components/Rail.tsx; demo/web/src/hooks/use-turn-stream.ts; demo/web/src/hooks/live-context.tsx.
- **Command palette** — severity: minor; fabricated: objectRefFromId maps `rev_`->review, but review-queue rows have NO `rev_` id prefix -- ReviewRow.id is a plain `number` (demo/web/src/api/types.ts:697). There is no `rev_` prefix anywhere in src/util/ids.ts. The spec's claim that `rev_` 'detail/why routes already exist' is false: reviews are numeric-keyed and only listed via GET /api/reviews; there is no per-review detail or why route., objectRefFromId maps `cd_`->creator_directive and asserts its detail/why routes exist. The real creator-directive prefix is `cdir_` (src/util/ids.ts:94, confirmed in data as `cdir_*`), not `cd_`. Additionally creator_directive is NOT a parseTarget kind (src/correction/service.ts:289-298 / src/util/ids.ts correctionTargetIdKindDescriptors), so getWhy CANNOT resolve it, and the only creator-directive routes are GET /api/creator-directives + POST .../revoke + .../supersede -- no detail/why route. Both the prefix string and the 'route already exists' claim are wrong., objectRefFromId maps `turn_`->turn->getLedger as if every turn id resolves. GET /api/turns/:id/ledger is real but serves only from the in-memory ledgerCache (demo/server/src/app.ts:2520-2528), populated by the evidence_ledger:built frame for turns that ran THIS process; historical/persisted turn ids return 404. 'open turn by id' will dead-end for most turn ids. Not a fabricated endpoint, but the spec overstates it as a general turn-detail route.; missing pivots: No pivot from a SESSION result into its turns/ledger or into the Stream screen scoped to that session -- selecting a session only switches the global ?session= param; the inspector model has no session->turn->ledger drill path surfaced., No pivot from an attachment (att_) result to its owning turn/stream entry or to the bytes view (attachmentBytesUrl / getAttachmentMetadata exist and could feed the inspector Evidence/Raw tabs)., No pivot from a turn (turn_) into the per-phase trace / live turn-phase timeline -- the rich turn:phase:* and turn:phase:detail frames feed the Cognition screen's turn view, an obvious inspector Timeline source the spec ignores., Open-by-ID omits semantic graph navigation: a semn_/seme_ result should pivot into the Graph screen (getSemanticGraph/getSemanticNode/getSemanticEdge) as a 'view in graph' source-screen pivot, not just the inspector drawer., No pivot from a commitment result into its revoke/supersede action or its creator-directive reconciliation review, despite those routes existing.
  - Verified against the real codebase. All 13 apiBindings map verbatim to verified catalog endpoints, and all 3 liveFrameBindings (maintenance:tick, stream:append, borg:reset) are real frames -- so NO load-bearing endpoint is fabricated in the binding lists themselves. Confirmed real: RAIL_ITEMS has exactly 9 routes (Rail.tsx:20-29) with glyph/short/num; correctionActionKind at Memory/index.tsx:86 maps ep_/semn_/seme_ (extraction target is sound and matches the 'grep before adding a helper' rule); Tag TagKind = acc|warn|bad|info|purple|solid (Tag.tsx:3); use-session SESSION_ID_PATTERN /^sess_.../ + replaceState/popstate setter (use-session.ts); Modal window keydown Escape pattern (Modal.tsx, lines ~11-24 not 17-24 -- trivial off-by); getMemoryBand(id,{query}) signature real (client.ts:296); App.tsx has route/setRoute/sessionId/setSessionId/sessions/activeAudience, the 409 'mark a creator first' operator guard (App.tsx:68), and onOpenReview={()=>setRoute('review')} (App.tsx:138,144); ChatInput textarea is at line 149 with onKeyDown at 164 (spec cited 164 -- close enough, file/element real).

CARDINAL-RULE CHECK: CLEAN. This is read-only operator navigation. Search binds to getMemoryBand({query}) (no audienceEntityId), renders disclosure/trust/taint as render-time Tags (label, not filter), and explicitly states results are recall-global. No output policing, no recall gating, no disclosure-as-filter. The opus5Verdict line is appropriate and accurate.

The defects are all in the design-body objectRefFromId prefix table, NOT in the declared apiBindings (which is why I rate this minor, not major -- no load-bearing declared endpoint is fabricated and no cardinal rule is violated). But the spec makes false factual claims to justify that table: it asserts rev_/cd_/cmt_/turn_/sess_/att_ prefixes 'whose detail/why routes already exist.' Three of those are wrong: (1) rev_ does not exist -- review ids are numeric (number), list-only, no detail/why route; (2) cd_ is the wrong string (real prefix cdir_) AND creator_directive is not a getWhy target AND has no detail route; (3) turn_->getLedger only works for turns whose ledger is in the live process cache, so it dead-ends for historical ids. The accurate open-by-id set is ep_/semn_/seme_/cmt_ (getWhy) + att_ (getAttachmentMetadata) + sess_ (session list); rev_ and cd_ should move into requiresBackendGap as 'per-object detail route needed.' Relevant files: demo/web/src/screens/Memory/index.tsx:86, src/correction/service.ts:289-298 + 612-766, src/util/ids.ts:82-122 + 289-298, demo/web/src/api/types.ts:696-705, demo/server/src/app.ts:2494/2520/2768-2794.
- **Cognitive Orrery — the hero visualization** — severity: none; missing pivots: Provenance 'why is this here' pivot omitted: the existing WhyDrawer + getWhy (GET /api/correction/:id/why, verified at client.ts:340, component at components/WhyDrawer.tsx) is the established drill-down from any object id to its provenance/evidence. The Provenance-mode lens entry and ring-body hover are the natural launch points; the spec routes Provenance via ledger source_type links but never names the existing getWhy/WhyDrawer pivot., Attachment inspector pivot omitted: OrreryObjectRef includes an 'attachment' kind and image_attachment is an EvidenceLedgerSourceType, and getAttachmentMetadata/getAttachmentStatuses/attachmentBytesUrl all exist (client.ts:587/595/677), but no pivot from a lens image-attachment entry into an attachment inspector is described., Identity drill-down for the self ring: belief_revision/identity_inconsistency faults pin to the self ring and getIdentity is bound, but the spec gives no explicit self-ring -> Identity screen / open-question / growth-marker pivot symmetric to the memory-band -> Atlas pivot., Stream pivot underspecified: GET /api/stream (getStream) is in apiBindings but eventTail (the orrery activity log) is sourced from use-turn-stream's WS cache, not getStream — getStream appears bound-but-unconsumed; either wire a stream-entry -> Stream-screen pivot or drop the binding.
  - VERDICT: clean (severity none). No fabricated load-bearing endpoints and no cardinal-rule violations.

FABRICATION CHECK — PASS. All 16 apiBindings resolve to verified client methods (getState, getStream, getTurns, getLedger, getMemoryBands, getSemanticGraph, getCommitments, getCreatorDirectives, getReviews, getDreamState, getSharedState, getIdentity, getSemanticNode, getSemanticEdge, getWhy). All 15 liveFrameBindings appear verbatim as type strings in api/types.ts AND are handled in hooks/use-turn-stream.ts. requiresBackendGap entries are honest and accurate — reasonable new needs are correctly parked there, not smuggled into bindings.

CARDINAL-RULE CHECK — PASS, and notably well-honored.
(a) No recall gating. getMemoryBands carries only an optional `session` param (verified client.ts:288) and the spec does NOT pass audience to it; rings render every band's count globally. Audience is used only as glow/ranking/annotation. 
(b) The decisive check: SharedStateCloud routes to getSharedState?audience= (GET /api/shared-state). Server impl (app.ts:3009) returns listEntriesForAudience(audience) — this IS the disclosure/common-ground surface, NOT a recall path. The spec renders clouds as a SEPARATE axis 'explicitly distinct from the global recall rings,' which is exactly the three-axis split (recall global / disclosure contextual / common-ground) the CARDINAL MEMORY RULE mandates. It does not pipe an audience-scoped endpoint into the recall rings.
(c) Disclosure labels (trust_rank, taint, citation_type, session_scope, origin_audience) are render-time only and sourced from EvidenceLedgerEntry (verified api/types.ts:936-953); used as labels, never as filters. No element rewrites/suppresses output; correction/review actions only deep-link to sanctioned endpoints (getWhy/postCorrectionForget/postCorrectionCorrect/patchCorrectionReview all verified). Opus 5.0 verdict (in-scope, presentation/surfacing gap) is correctly applied.

STRUCTURAL KEYING — PASS. Every visual encoding keys on verified structural enums, not words: CommitmentEnforcement = 'critical'|'advisory' (matches arc tiers); CreatorDirectiveActivationScope (same_as_disclosure/operator_only/public/allow_list/subject_only/all_except) + activation_allowed/excluded_entity_ids (matches beacon cones); CreatorDirectiveStatus = active|superseded|revoked; all 7 ReviewKind values map to the documented fault-node targets; the 8 MemoryBandId values match the 8 rings. Language-agnostic.

MINOR FIELD-NAME SLIPS (implementation detail, not fabrication, intent intact): (1) Orrery props say `dream: DreamStateResponse` and modes reference `dream.process` / 'active dream process', but the real shape is `processes: DreamProcessSummary[]` (api/types.ts:800). (2) ConstraintArc tiers reference `commitment.enforcement` but the field is `enforcement_class: CommitmentEnforcement` (api/types.ts:466). Fix the field names at implementation time.

Severity stays NONE: no load-bearing endpoint/frame is fabricated and no cardinal rule is violated. The missing-pivot items and field-name slips are quality notes, not blockers.
- **Design system & visual language** — severity: minor; missing pivots: Section 9 inspector pivots reference getSemanticNode / getSemanticEdge (GET /api/semantic/nodes/:id, GET /api/semantic/edges/:id) and getAttachmentMetadata, but apiBindings omits the two semantic node/edge endpoints. They EXIST in the catalog and are the natural pivot targets for semn_/seme_ ids rendered as navigable chips (section 9 inspector-out), so they should be in apiBindings. Not a fabrication -- a missing binding for a pivot the spec itself describes., MiniOrrery click 'pivots to the turn inspector for the active turn' (section 7/9) and SeverityChip pivots to 'the affected object', but no turn-history binding is declared. GET /api/turns (getTurns) is in the catalog and is the obvious turn-inspector backing read; it is neither bound nor named as the pivot target. The turn inspector destination is left undefined., Inspector-out (section 9) lists source_stream_id / turn_id / semn_/seme_/ep_ as navigable, but omits pivots for the other EvidenceLedgerSourceType ids the ledger surfaces -- commitment, creator-directive, shared_state, action_record, relational_slot. commitment -> GET /api/commitments and creator-directive -> GET /api/creator-directives are already bound, so those pivots are cheap and obviously expected from a ledger row but are not wired in the cross-link map., DisclosureLabel/CountBadge expose object id+kind to pivot via getWhy(id) (GET /api/correction/:id/why), but no pivot is offered into the correction/forget/correct affordances that the same /api/correction/:id object supports -- the inspector-from-disclosure path stops at 'why' and omits the natural hop to the correction review surface.
  - FABRICATION: clean. All 15 apiBindings appear verbatim in the verified catalog (confirmed against demo/web/src/api/client.ts) and all 11 liveFrameBindings appear verbatim in the verified WS frame catalog (and are all actually consumed in demo/web/src). requiresBackendGap was declared [] -- that is the spec's main inaccuracy: the DisclosureLabel data-surfacing + 6->4 disclosure-class mapping is real work that should have been listed there (details in requiresBackendGap above), so the 'zero backend gap, pure presentation' framing is overstated for that one component.

CARDINAL RULE: clean, and notably careful. The design is explicitly anti-recall-gating. Section 5 hard-constraints state 'Labels annotate; they never filter' and 'Current-audience may only rank/boost ordering... never remove a row,' and require full recalled sets rendered with labels on Atlas/Ledger/Stream/graph/identity. Disclosure-scope, taint, and trust are render-time labels/ranking axes only -- exactly the FINE case in the task. SeverityChip derives rank structurally from ReviewKind/enforcement_class (verified those are structural fields, not word-matches) and is explicitly forbidden from hiding rows. No output-policing affordance: the vocabulary exposes only sanctioned correction/review endpoints; the one-shot critical-review pulse is a presentation cue, not suppression. The cross-audience purple marker is a render-time label over existing origin_audience provenance (133 backend refs incl. src/cognition/disclosure-labels.ts), consistent with CLAUDE.md rule 6's 'label, not a new known-to mechanism.' Fail-closed-to-unknown is asserted.

CARDINAL-RULE check PASSES (no issues). (a) No recall gating: the inspector is operator-facing presentation/navigation over rows that already exist; disclosure labels (origin_audience, private/operator, trust_rank, taint, citation_type) are explicitly rendered as labels-not-filters, empty/needs_backend pivots are shown visible-but-disabled ('never hidden, so absence is legible') which is the opposite of audience-hiding. The hint.audience on ObjectRef and the shared-state ?audience= fetch are disclosure-scoped reads of a per-audience object (shared_state is audience-keyed by schema) parameterizing a fetch, NOT a gate over the entity's cognition recall -- consistent with the cardinal memory rule (audience as a fetch/disclosure parameter on operator tooling, not a cognition recall predicate). (b) No output policing: the only mutating pivots are the already-sanctioned correct/forget/invalidate/revoke/supersede/revert/review-resolution endpoints; nothing rewrites or suppresses entity output. (c) No disclosure-as-recall-filter: labels render at the Evidence tab beside chips, never filter what's shown. Type detection keys on structural id prefixes + schema field names, explicitly NOT word-matching user content (idRefsFromFields walks known structural id-bearing fields, refuses to regex arbitrary tokens in free text) -- language-agnostic, satisfies cardinal rule 2. Opus 5.0 verdict (in-scope, presentation/navigation gap) is correct and well-argued.

Severity = none: no fabricated endpoints, no cardinal-rule violation. The findings are minor pivot omissions only (see missingPivots), which the schema records but which do not rise to minor severity for the overall area since the spec's reliability tiering (direct/in_list/needs_backend) and backend-gap routing are sound and honest. One small internal inconsistency worth fixing before build: the `mood` ObjectType is declared but has no fetcher or pivot path, so it should either be dropped or explicitly marked needs_backend like the other numeric (audit/review) types.
- **Mission Control** — severity: minor; missing pivots: Inspector ledger-entry pivot map drops two real EvidenceLedgerSourceType values. The spec maps ledger entry -> episode/semantic_node/semantic_edge/commitment/shared_state, but EvidenceLedgerSourceType (demo/web/src/api/types.ts:921) also includes 'action_record' and 'relational_slot'. Both are valid ledger sources that a provenance-mode/Inspector pivot should target; as written they have no pivot. Add action_record -> action-state and relational_slot -> relational/social pivots., image_attachment ledger source not wired to the attachment Inspector target. Provenance mode reads EvidenceLedger.sections[].entries[].source_type and the Inspector defines an 'attachment' kind, but the ledger-entry pivot list omits source_type==='image_attachment' -> attachment. Minor inconsistency to close., No pivot from the orrery semantic memory rings directly into a semantic_node/semantic_edge Inspector view — ring click only does setRoute('memory') to the Atlas. Defensible as a design choice, but a node/edge deep-link (the catalog has GET /api/semantic/nodes/:id and /edges/:id) would complete the symmetry with the other orrery overlays that do open the Inspector.
  - FABRICATION CHECK: clean. All 18 apiBindings map verbatim to verified-catalog REST endpoints, and all corresponding frontend client methods exist in demo/web/src/api/client.ts (getState/getSessions/getMemoryBands/getReviews[open_only]/getCommitments/getCreatorDirectives/getDreamState/getStream[kind+session filter]/getLedger/getSemanticGraph/getPrompts/getAttachmentStatuses[?ids=]/getAttachmentMetadata/openOperatorSession/setSessionPolicy/setCreatorByName/getCreatorEntity/getWhy). All 16 liveFrameBindings are real frame literal types in demo/web/src/api/types.ts:1126-1215. The use-turn-stream handle props the orrery consumes (flowSnapshotByTurn, ledgerByTurn, eventTail, activeTurnId, lastPhase, terminalOutcome, delibPath, finalAttempt) all exist (use-turn-stream.ts:465-476), and the Xray tracePlaceholder stub is real (Xray.tsx:18,37). FlowChart/ParticleField are exported props-driven components; ParticipationPolicyControl exists (Cognition/index.tsx:152); SemanticTopology force engine is correctly NOT pulled in. App.tsx:40 default route 'cognition' confirmed. No fabricated endpoints or frames.\n\nTWO MINOR ACCURACY ISSUES (not fabrications, do not change severity to major):\n(1) The 'reused components' list cites 'SemanticEdgeDetail.tsx' as a real file path alongside WhyDrawer.tsx/SemanticNodeDetail.tsx/SupersededByChip.tsx (those three ARE standalone exported component files under demo/web/src/components/). But SemanticEdgeDetail is a LOCAL, non-exported function inside demo/web/src/screens/Review/index.tsx:515 — not a shared component file. The symbol is real but reusing it in the Inspector requires extracting it first; the spec implies it is already a peer reusable file. Minor.\n(2) Spec card 6/7 prose describes AttachmentMetadataResponse.status values as ('quarantined','active','stream_active','parent_active'). Actually status is an OBJECT OF BOOLEANS {active:boolean; quarantined:boolean; stream_active?:boolean; parent_active?:boolean} (types.ts:186-191). Field names are right; the rendering must read booleans, not a string enum. Minor.\n\nCARDINAL-RULE CHECK: clean — no major violation.\n(a) No recall/cognition gating by audience or session. useAttentionData binds getMemoryBands() and getCommitments() with NO audience/session arg (both client methods accept optional session/audience params but the spec deliberately omits them), and the compliance section explicitly states the orrery memory rings 'show the full bands regardless of who is in the active session.' Session/audience is used only as a ranking/label and to scope the operator-telemetry outcome card (which reads the conversation stream, not recall). Mission Control touches nothing in src/cognition|retrieval|memory|offline — it is pure frontend presentation of already-broadcast runtime state, so it structurally cannot introduce a backend recall gate.\n(b) No output policing. Card 5 uses lib/stream-outcomes.ts streamOutcomeSummary, verified as a pure read-only classifier over already-emitted StreamEntry rows (observes/labels suppression reason; never rewrites/suppresses/re-gates). The spec routes all correction/repair affordances through sanctioned deep-screen endpoints and only links to them.\n(c) Disclosure/provenance/taint/trust (origin_audience, private/operator, trust_rank, taint, citation_type, audience_role, participation_policy, commitment.audience) are all rendered as render-time labels/chips, not used as recall filters. Directive-conflict detection (card 3) is id-overlap/superseded-by structural correlation, not phrase matching — language-agnostic, consistent with the multilingual rule.\n\nOverall: the design is API-honest and cardinal-clean. Severity is MINOR only because of the two accuracy slips (non-exported SemanticEdgeDetail mislabeled as a file path; attachment status object-vs-enum) and the dropped action_record/relational_slot Inspector pivots. None of these fabricate a load-bearing endpoint or violate a cardinal rule, so this is not major. opus5Verdict ('in-scope, presentation/surfacing only, zero recall-gating, zero output-policing, only true backend need is a non-recall attachment list/count endpoint') is accurate and well-supported.
- **Conversation Workbench** — severity: major; fabricated: GET /api/turn -- NOT in the verified catalog. The catalog has POST /api/turn (submit a turn) and GET /api/turns (history). The server only registers app.post("/api/turn"), app.get("/api/turns"), and app.get("/api/turns/:id/ledger") (demo/server/src/app.ts:2508,2520,3482). The design body itself only ever uses postTurn (POST) for the Send path, so this is almost certainly a typo for POST /api/turn rather than a load-bearing fabricated read endpoint -- but as written it does not appear verbatim in the catalog and must be corrected to POST /api/turn.; missing pivots: Attachment pivot is asymmetric: the spec maps ledger source_type image_attachment -> Inspector attachment and binds GET /api/attachments/:id + /bytes, but does not specify the inbound pivot FROM an attachment back to the turn(s) that cited it, nor a lane-row image thumbnail -> attachment-metadata pivot (getAttachmentMetadata is bound but only described for the right-pane). Worth an explicit attachment<->turn cross-link., Shared State tab (tab 2) and Commitments tab (tab 4) are read views, but the spec routes commitment pivots to 'Inspector/Governance' while shared-state rows get no outbound pivot at all -- a shared_state ledger entry pivots to Inspector, but a row in the Shared State tab itself (from getSharedState) has no stated pivot target. Inconsistent., Open Questions tab (tab 5, from getIdentity().open_questions) is explicitly read-only with 'actions live in the Inspector', but no pivot is specified from an open-question row INTO the Inspector/Identity screen (PATCH /api/identity/open-questions/:id is the real resolve/abandon/bump endpoint). The pivot target for acting on an open question is left dangling., No pivot from a turn's delib/final token activity or a phase node to the Prompt Lab / assembled-prompt context beyond tab 6's generic 'Link to Prompt Lab' -- the phase->ledger cross-highlight is specified but the phase->prompt-block linkage (which framing block drove a phase) is omitted, even though getAssembledPrompt section tags are surfaced., Inbound deep-links are listed for Mission Control and Stream, but no inbound pivot is specified from the Memory band browser or Semantic Graph screens (which can surface an episode/node/edge id) INTO the Workbench to see 'which turn last recalled this' -- the natural reverse of the ledger episode/semantic_node/semantic_edge -> Inspector pivots.
  - VERDICT: major, driven solely by (1) the fabricated 'GET /api/turn' binding and (2) the entire pivot layer binding to a Universal Inspector / onInspect / InspectorKind that does not exist in the codebase and is presented as already-wired App.tsx infrastructure. Both are correctable and neither is a cardinal-rule violation.

CARDINAL RULES: CLEAN. (a) Recall is not gated: the design explicitly renders session_scope/taint/citation_type/persistence_class as Tag chips with the stated rule 'never as filter predicates'; the existing LedgerView (demo/web/src/screens/Cognition/LedgerView.tsx) renders all sections/entries with no audience filter and the spec preserves that; the `audience` prop is used only for shared-state fetch and attachment-bytes transport scoping (disclosure), not for filtering recalled rows. Retrieved-Memory tab derives from via_retrieval on the same globally-recalled ledger, not a separate audience-scoped fetch. (b) No output policing: only write paths are postTurn and setSessionPolicy; correct/forget/invalidate are pivots into the (to-be-built) Inspector routing to sanctioned correction endpoints, never inline rewrite/suppress. (c) Suppression/observed reasons use the structural lib/stream-outcomes.ts classifier (SuppressionOutcomeClass / streamOutcomeSummary), participation meanings are static UI copy keyed on the enum value -- no content-word matching. The opus5Verdict is accurate.

All 4 liveFrameBindings (stream:append, evidence_ledger:built, maintenance:tick, borg:reset) appear verbatim in the verified WS-frame catalog. requiresBackendGap is honest and verified against actual code -- the spec correctly refuses to invent server params for status/structural/date/search filters and forward-pagination.

CARDINAL-RULE: clean. This is correctly scoped as an operator-facing append-only LOG/provenance viewer, not a cognition path. Audience/kind/status/structural filters select which already-emitted rows are SHOWN in the table; they never gate what the entity may internally recall. Audience is explicitly labeled a 'view filter' and rendered as a disclosure Tag, not a recall predicate. taint/trust/citation_type/quarantine/source_message_key all render as render-time labels. No output policing -- read-only inspection + pivots; corrections explicitly deferred to other screens. Filters key on schema fields (turn_id, kind, turn_status, compressed, source_message_key, attachment_id), never on matching words in content -- language-agnostic, no English/keyword lists. The cardinal-rule self-defense in the spec is sound and matches what current StreamScreen already does.

TWO MINOR ACCURACY DEFECTS (degradable, not fabricated bindings):
1. The ProvenanceInspector says 'related ledger ... from the live ledgerByTurn cache via context.' This is wrong about the wiring: ledgerByTurn lives on the useTurnStream hook return (use-turn-stream.ts:475,498), NOT on the LiveEvents context (use-live-events.ts) that this screen consumes via useLiveEventsContext() (Stream/index.tsx:501). To get the live ledger cache the screen would have to instantiate useTurnStream itself. The spec DOES offer 'or lazy getLedger(turn_id)' as fallback, and getLedger + the evidence_ledger:built frame are both real, so this is functional -- but the 'via context' path as described does not exist and should be corrected to 'lazy getLedger, optionally a local useTurnStream cache.'
2. Path imprecision: spec cites 'screen-utils.ts' for shortId/fieldLabel/etc.; actual path is demo/web/src/screens/screen-utils.ts. lib/stream-utils.ts and lib/stream-outcomes.ts paths are correct.

Net: structurally sound, honest about gaps, cardinal-rule compliant. Severity minor for the ledgerByTurn-via-context mis-wiring claim plus the omitted pivots (image_attachment ledger source -> local AttachmentViewer; correction/why pivot; explicit Cognition->Stream reciprocal deep-link). Nothing load-bearing is fabricated, so not major.
- **Memory Atlas** — severity: none; missing pivots: Reverse pivot OUT to the Conversation Workbench turn ledger is omitted — GET /api/turns/:id/ledger exists and EvidenceLedgerSourceType (types.ts:938) carries source_type, but the spec only wires Workbench→Atlas, not band-item→which-turn-ledger-cited-it., No Inspector Evidence path for affective/social provenance — MoodHistoryEntry.provenance (types.ts:344, Record<string,unknown> with stream-entry refs) and social entity history are not given a source-evidence pivot the way episodic/semantic/relational are. Minor (those bands lack correction endpoints anyway)., Episode→derived-semantic pivot is asserted ('derived semantic nodes/edges' under episode pivots) but no backing field exists on EpisodeMemoryItem; it implicitly leans on the same client-side 300-node graph reconstruction already flagged as backend gap (5), rather than a real edge. Worth calling out so it is not mistaken for a wired pivot.

Field-level claims also check out: EpisodeMemoryItem lineage+source fields; SemanticMemoryNode/Edge invalidated_* (types.ts:272-275) + source_episode_ids/evidence_episode_ids; ProceduralMemoryItem alpha/beta (323-324, backs the Beta-posterior bar); MoodSnapshot half_life_hours/recent_triggers (994-995); MoodHistoryEntry trigger_reason/provenance (343-344); SocialMemoryItem trust/attachment/interaction_count; RelationalMemoryItem state/contradicted_count/alternate_count/name_provenance. CARDINAL RULE (recall global, disclosure contextual): the design is not just compliant — it is structurally enforced by the existing API surface. getMemoryBand (client.ts:296-316) accepts only {session,limit,cursor,query}; there is NO audience parameter, so the audience control CANNOT thread a recall filter even if someone tried. The design explicitly captions the audience control 'ranking only — recall is global, never filtered' and asserts in tests that no audience param reaches getMemoryBand. Disclosure labels (audience/taint/state/confidence/trust_rank) render as Tags at view time, shown never used to hide rows — matches CARDINAL MEMORY RULE clauses 2-3 exactly. Correction (why/forget/correct/invalidate) is the sanctioned host-boundary path operating on stored objects by id, never rewriting emitted text — not output policing. No deterministic output gating anywhere. The opus5Verdict line is present and accurate.

ONE WATCH ITEM (not a violation): both getMemoryBands and getMemoryBand accept a `session` param and the screen threads sessionId. Session is a legitimate view-scope axis for an operator-inspection read of derived substrate (admin/disclosure-side UI, not the entity's cognition path governed by CognitionRetrievalOptions). It is NOT an audience gate, and the API shape already prevents an audience gate. Flagging only so implementers keep session as a view-scope convenience and never let it drift into a 'what the entity may know' predicate.

MISSING PIVOTS are minor and arguably covered by the generic onOpenInspector({type,id}) contract; severity stays none."

A 'queued -- view in review queue' link is the obvious missing affordance., Commitment cross-link: open questions and goals can relate to commitments (the correction service treats commitment as a peer identity-adjacent target with recordType commitment), but the spec has no pivot to the Commitments screen / GET /api/commitments. Minor since commitments are a separate facet., Inbound from Cognition ledger: a turn's evidence ledger frequently cites self-band values/goals/open-questions; no pivot from a ledger citation into the Studio object is noted (Cognition -> Studio).

API FABRICATION CHECK -- CLEAN. All 9 apiBindings appear verbatim in the catalog (GET /api/identity; POST /api/identity/values; POST /api/identity/goals; PATCH /api/identity/goals/:id; POST /api/identity/growth-markers; PATCH /api/identity/open-questions/:id; GET /api/correction/:id/why; POST /api/correction/:id/forget; POST /api/correction/:id/correct). All 3 liveFrameBindings (maintenance:tick, dream:process:completed, borg:reset) appear verbatim in the verified WS-frame catalog. Verified end-to-end: demo/server/src/app.ts:3063-3098 wires why/forget/correct by passing the raw :id straight to input.borg.correction.* with no server-side prefix filter; the refetch-on-frame pattern (live.subscribe -> if frame.type==='maintenance:tick'|'dream:process:completed' void refetch()) is exactly what Review (index.tsx:1009) and Dream (index.tsx:220-270) already do, so the live bindings are idiomatic.

FALSE/MIS-SCOPED BACKEND GAP (the load-bearing finding). The spec's requiresBackendGap[2] and [3] assert the correction id-prefix router 'only routes ep_/semn_/seme_' and that value/goal/trait/open_question (and growth/period) all need new prefix routing. That premise is FALSE. src/correction/service.ts parseTarget() (line 77) iterates correctionTargetIdKindDescriptors (src/util/ids.ts:289), which ALREADY contains episode, semantic_node, semantic_edge, value, goal, trait, commitment, open_question. The resolveTargetMetadata switch (l.264), the forget switch (l.425), the correct/why switch (l.615) all fully handle value/goal/trait/open_question. So why/correct/forget for values, goals, traits, AND open questions resolve today with zero backend work -- and the spec's separate 'Goal correct/forget parity' gap entry is entirely spurious. The genuinely-unrouted prefixes are ONLY growth_marker (grw_) and autobiographical_period (abp_), which are absent from the descriptor table. Net effect of the error: the spec over-claims missing backend work for 4 object types (would burn effort re-adding routing that exists) while still correctly flagging the 2 that are real (grw_/abp_) plus trait-add and autobiography-edit. The IdentityEvent record_type field is a free string in the FE type, but that's display only; routing is by id prefix, which is what matters.

CARDINAL-RULE CHECK -- CLEAN. (a) Recall gating: none. The spec's Architectural-posture paragraph explicitly states the screen never filters/hides a value/goal/trait/question/marker/period by audience/session, and renders source/source_process/provenance as origin/disclosure LABELS (Tag chips + why? drawer), never as recall gates. This is consistent with how the live correction service computes disclosureLabel (selfMemoryCorrectionDisclosureLabel etc.) as a label, not a filter. (b) Output policing: none -- the only mutations are sanctioned identity-lifecycle writes (the existing DirectWriteNotice ack-gated host-boundary path, preserved verbatim) and correction routes; no deterministic scoring/rewriting/suppression of model output. (c) Label-as-filter: the SelfRings 'pulse on recent change' keys off IdentityEvent ts (data), grouping keys on state/status/record_type enums and ids -- language-agnostic, structural, no surface-word matching. Good.

DATA-FIELD CHECK -- CLEAN. demo/web/src/api/types.ts:347-446 confirms every rendered field exists: IdentityValue/IdentityTrait both carry state:'candidate'|'established' (the grouping axis), confidence, support_count, contradiction_count, evidence_episode_ids; trait also has strength. IdentityGoal has status enum + progress_notes + target_at + priority. OpenQuestion has urgency, goal_id, source, unresolved_rumination_ticks, last_ruminated_at, status. GrowthMarker has category/what_changed/before_description/after_description/source_process. AutobiographicalPeriod has narrative/themes/key_episode_ids/start_ts/end_ts. The spec's claim that Identity currently hides trait contradiction_count while Memory shows it is true (Memory/index.tsx:233 renders it; Identity index.tsx:780 shows only support_count).

Tag (TagKind acc/warn/bad/info/purple/solid/''), Modal, Empty, Spark, Panel (.panel/.panel-header/.panel-body), WhyDrawer (+ getWhy via api/client), SupersededByChip all exist with the claimed exports. The in-Identity helpers it claims to move (DirectWriteNotice, directWriteAcknowledged gate, DirectPatchLabel/Notice, IdentityCorrectionButtons, OpenQuestionEventsSection, IdentityEmptyBand, eventActionTag/provenanceLabel/eventRecordSummary) all exist in the current index.tsx as claimed. MINOR PRECEDENT IMPRECISION. The spec says SelfRings/refetch 'mirrors how SemanticTopology refetches on tick.' It cannot 'pivot into the Universal Inspector' as specced; it has no routing capability and the target screen does not exist.; CARDINAL: No recall-gating violation. Nothing reads the entity's emitted text and decides whether it reaches a user. getWhy is read-only provenance inspection (GET /api/correction/:id/why), correctly labeled as inspection-not-gating.; Disclosure/provenance handling is render-time labeling only (audit targets, source tags, reverter chips), not recall filtering -- compliant.; One correct guardrail the spec keeps: belief-revision rows offer ONLY patchReviewItem(id,{action:'dismiss'}) inline and route accept/reject to Review -- matches that PATCH /api/dream/review/:id is the dismiss-only belief-revision endpoint.; missing pivots: The spec's stated cross-link target (Universal Inspector) is itself missing/non-existent, so all the listed pivots are moot until a real target screen is named. With the Topbar reset gone, no screen other than the rail glyph routes to Admin; worth an explicit inbound nav from at least Dream/Topbar., active_session pivot: spec says it 'opens the session in the inspector' but does not name the actual mechanism (onNavigate to which route + how the inspector receives the session id). # Appendix C — Completeness review

I have what I need. Synthesizing the cross-cutting completeness review.

# Borg Operator-UI Redesign — Whole-Plan Completeness Review

The plan is per-area sound (most verdicts clean/minor, no cardinal-rule drift), but it was reviewed *area-by-area*. The gaps that matter live in the seams. The single biggest one: **the Universal Inspector is a hard prerequisite for 9 of 12 screens but is itself only "designed," never sequenced — and three areas already failed their own verdict (major) for binding to it as if it exists.**

## Must-fix gaps

- **Universal Inspector is an unbuilt, unsequenced dependency that the entire plan's cross-linking value rests on.** Mission Control, Workbench, Stream, Memory Atlas, Identity, Governance, Review, Dream, Prompt Lab all bind their pivots to `openInspector`/`onInspect`/`{type,id}`. It does not exist in `demo/web/src` (grep = 0 hits) and is not in the `RouteId` union. Dream Ops, Workbench, and Prompt Lab were each rated **major** solely for treating it as existing infra. Fix: declare the Inspector + `objectRegistry` + the `InspectorProvider`/`openObject(ref)` App-level seam as **Phase 0**, built and merged before any screen migration. Every other screen's pivot section is non-functional until it lands.
- **`onInspect`/`openObject(ref)` contract is specified four different ways across areas — pick one before building.** Shell says `openInspector(ref)` + a `?view=` deep-link target threaded from App; Cross-linking model says `openObject({type,id})` via a `LiveEventsContext`-style provider; Inspector drawer says a context holding a "target stack" + `setRoute` escape hatch; Workbench/Stream/Mission Control say an `onInspect({kind,id})` *prop callback*. Prop-threading vs context-provider vs URL-param are mutually incompatible wiring models. Two areas claim `{kind,id}`, two claim `{type,id}`. Rationale: the Inspector is the shared spine; divergent contracts guarantee rework and inconsistent deep-linking.
- **The per-session live-pulse transport gap is real and recurs in 3 areas, but is only flagged in one.** Verified: server `shouldDeliver` (live.ts:274) gates `stream:append` to `subscribedSessions`; the client subscribes to exactly one session at a time and **never sends `subscribe_global`** (grep = 0), though the server supports it and defaults `subscribedGlobal=true` for global frames only. So the SessionFleet/Mission-Control "live recency pulse per row" silently works for the active session only. Shell flagged it (its sole "major" driver); **Mission Control's `SessionFleet` and the `useLiveCache` session-activity index restate the same assumption without acknowledging the gap.** Fix once, centrally: either have the client emit `subscribe_global` (cheapest — server already handles it) or derive fleet recency from `last_activity_at` refetch deltas. Decide before three screens encode the false assumption.
- **No global error/empty/loading consistency strategy, despite 12 screens and a new shell.** Today every screen hand-rolls `<div className="notice">loading X</div>` / `notice bad`. Only `Empty.tsx` is shared; there is **no shared Loading or Error component and no ErrorBoundary anywhere in the app** (confirmed). The plan adds ~7 new multi-pane screens plus the Inspector (6 tabs, each an independent fetch) and a command palette, multiplying ad-hoc states. Admin/Runtime proposes the *first* `AppErrorBoundary` but scopes it to its own error-log card. Fix: promote a shared `Loading`/`ErrorState` pair + a top-level ErrorBoundary as shell infrastructure, consumed everywhere, before screens fan out. Otherwise the Inspector's per-tab loading/404 (e.g. `LedgerView`'s "ledger not retained pre-restart") will be styled 12 different ways.
- **Three "reuse verbatim" components are actually local non-exported functions requiring extraction — and four areas double-claim them.** `SemanticEdgeDetail` is claimed as a shared `components/` file by the Inspector, Mission Control, Memory Atlas, and Review — it does **not exist as a file**; it is local to `Review/index.tsx:515` (and edges render inline in `Memory/index.tsx`). Same for `OpenQuestionEventsSection` (local in `Identity/index.tsx:256`) and `WhyDrawer`'s `JsonValueView` (local, line 21). Multiple areas list these under "reused, not rewritten," understating real extraction work and creating ownership ambiguity (who extracts `SemanticEdgeDetail` — Review, Memory, or Inspector?). Fix: a single "extract-to-shared" migration ticket, owned once, sequenced with Phase 0.

## Should-address

- **Cross-corpus search and unified object-lookup-by-ID are deferred backend gaps that two user-facing primitives are *built on*, not just enhanced by.** The Command Palette's "search memory/stream" and "open object by ID," and the Inspector's raw-id resolution, both degrade hard without `GET /api/object/:id` and a unified search endpoint. The plan correctly quarantines these in `requiresBackendGap`, but **never consolidates the deferred-gap list into one dependency ledger with a ship/no-ship decision.** Scattered across 8 areas are: unified object lookup, related-object endpoint, list-all-attachments (Mission Control's quarantined-attachments card has *no* count source), stream fetch-by-entry-ids/by-turn-id, per-band correction for procedural/affective/social/relational, `grw_`/`abp_` correction routing (the *only* genuinely-missing identity prefixes — Identity over-claimed 4 others that already route), prompt-revision-history, persisted phase traces (turn replay after refresh), per-turn duration, and per-run dream budget metering. Rationale: without one consolidated table, each screen ships a different degraded experience for the same missing capability, and the "open by ID" prefix tables disagree (Command Palette wrongly claims `rev_`/`cd_` routes exist; real is numeric reviews + `cdir_`).
- **No keyboard-nav or accessibility strategy across the new surfaces.** Existing a11y is shallow (67 aria/keydown hits, mostly `Modal` Escape + `Rail` labels). The plan adds a ⌘K command palette (the *only* keyboard-first surface), a right-side drawer with 6 tabs, a hero SVG Orrery with clickable bodies, kanban/matrix/scatter views, and three-pane cockpits — none specify focus management, tab order, drawer focus-trap/return, Escape semantics, or SVG-node keyboard reachability. Rationale: the Orrery and Inspector are click-only as specced; an operator console used for repair work needs keyboard reachability for every pivot the palette claims to expose.
- **Performance with large stream/memory sets is asserted "works at demo scale" but several cards do unbounded full-list client scans.** Mission Control's `AttentionQueue` derives 7 cards from full `getReviews`/`getCommitments`/`getCreatorDirectives` fetches; the Governance Scope Matrix is "client-side aggregation over already-fetched data"; Stream does turn-grouping + all structural filters client-side over the loaded window (server filters only session/kind/audience/cursor). Server defaults are `limit=50` with cursor pagination (verified), so these views see only a window, not the corpus — meaning counts and matrices are **silently lower-bounds** past 50 rows, with no "showing first N / count_is_lower_bound" affordance specified. Rationale: at real-memory scale (the live system holds 76 active + 30 archived episodes and growing) the matrices/counts mislead the operator without a stated windowing/aggregate contract.
- **Live-reconnect / post-reset cache invalidation is under-specified for the new shell-owned `useLiveCache`.** `borg:reset` triggers `window.location.reload()` today, but the plan introduces a persistent shell-level cache, a 64-LRU `flowSnapshotByTurn`, the Inspector's target stack, and a command-palette index — none specify what survives reconnect vs reset. Admin/Runtime even proposes a net-new `turnStream.resetCaches()` that nothing else references. Rationale: a stale Inspector target or fleet cache after a reset/reconnect shows the operator deleted objects as live.
- **Test strategy for the new architecture is unaddressed.** 19 existing tests cover hooks + per-screen smoke (`P2Screens.test.tsx`, etc.). The plan deletes per-screen detail panes and centralizes everything through the Inspector + `objectRegistry` + id-prefix sniffer, but names no test plan for: the registry's resolver table, the prefix→type router (the load-bearing language-agnostic discovery path), Inspector tab-capability visibility, or the global/per-session WS subscription change. Rationale: the prefix router and registry are the new single points of failure; they need the test coverage the deleted bespoke panes implicitly had.

## Nice-to-have

- **Orphaned backend capability: `POST /api/entities` (generic entity create) and `POST /api/entities/:id/borg-role` are in the catalog but no design consumes them.** Governance's "Sessions & entities controls" only mentions creator marking (`setCreatorByName`). Generic non-creator entity creation and arbitrary borg-role clearing have no UI home. Rationale: either Governance's entity tab should expose them or they should be noted as intentionally operator-CLI-only.
- **Field-shape slips to fix at implementation time (non-blocking, already caught per-area):** Orrery uses `dream.process` (real: `processes: DreamProcessSummary[]`) and `commitment.enforcement` (real: `enforcement_class`); Mission Control renders attachment `status` as a string enum (real: object of booleans `{active,quarantined,stream_active,parent_active}`); the `mood` ObjectType in the registry has no fetcher/pivot (drop or mark needs-backend). Rationale: trivial individually, but the attachment-status object-vs-enum slip appears in both Mission Control and Stream, so fix it in the shared attachment viewer once.
- **Reciprocal deep-links are specified one-directionally in places.** Stream is named "the canonical deep-link target for stream-id chips" but the Cognition→Stream reciprocal isn't wired; Prompt Lab expects inbound "fix is a better prompt" handoffs from Review/Dream that those areas don't explicitly emit. Rationale: minor wiring, but worth a single cross-link matrix so every "open in source screen" has a declared source and target.

**Cardinal-rule drift: none found.** Every area was verified clean — disclosure/audience/trust/taint render as labels and ranking boosts, never recall filters; the audience-parameterized reads (`getSharedState?audience=`, `getCommitments({audience})`) are correctly scoped to disclosure/common-ground/action-auth, not cognition recall; the API shape itself (`getMemoryBand` has no audience param) structurally prevents a recall gate. No output-policing surfaces: all mutations route through sanctioned correction/review/governance endpoints, never inline rewrite/suppression. The plan holds the line.
